How did the Ministry of Finance behave about these foreign exchange options? It did not only hold back, as we can see from Minister Rostowski's example, the work on the implementation of this directive, but also did not treat the costs of entrepreneurs incurred in purchasing foreign exchange options as the cost of doing business. So Polish companies were hit twice. Firstly, they had no protection, and secondly, when the tax office applied to them, the expenditure on currency options was not considered as costs, which caused many of them to go bankrupt. Many of them must have made deals with the banks. What's happened in the last eight years? The level of state revenue, as a share of the gross domestic product, is lower than 8 years ago. The sector's tax revenue still amounted to 22% in 2007 gross domestic product, to be precise - 22,7% , when they were only 19,8% in 2015. This means a loss of tax revenue of almost 3% GDP. Of course, one can wonder and argue that everyone had such problems, because Europe went through a period of economic crisis, but when we compare what happened in Poland with losses in other countries, we see that the greater loss of income occurred only in Cyprus, which practically collapsed, and Lithuania. After initial hesitation in many European countries, tax revenues have returned to previous levels. Thus, the average ratio of tax revenue in GDP in the European Union fell to 25% in 2009 GDP of the averaged countries of the Union, but this relationship has already returned to the level recorded before the recession in 2014. However, in our country, despite the macroeconomic situation which has been relatively better compared to other countries of the European Union, this relationship has been significantly reduced. Significantly, 2/3 of this decrease was due to negative trends in the area of indirect taxes, mainly VAT and excise duties. Only two other European countries have seen a deeper decline in indirect taxation to GDP - again in Cyprus and Ireland. Data relating to basic spot taxes, i.e. central budget revenue taxes, and taking into account the shares of local government units indicate that this decrease was even greater and reached 3.2 percentage points. If we take this 3.2 percentage point and multiply by the nominal gross domestic product, we see what kind of money we're talking about. This is a loss year to year in the order of PLN 60 billion. The biggest decrease in revenue ratio occurred in the case, as I mentioned, VAT and CIT, less in the case of PIT. Why smaller for PITs? For example, because the tax-free amount has not been valued. In this case, the powers of the President of the Council of Ministers to carry out supervision are to be transferred to the Minister responsible for Economic Affairs. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-11-sejm-18-1.u436	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! The novel, which we are talking about, unfortunately does not bring new solutions that we could expect in the context of the implementation of the economic programme by Prime Minister Morawiecki. It does not contain solutions expected by Polish companies, by entrepreneurs, which would allow e.g. extend the scope of the guarantees or guarantees given to the State Treasury. Nor are the necessary procedural simplifications in terms of guarantees and guarantees and changes in this respect proposed in my view. We have some detailed comments on this novel. First of all, it transfers competences to the minister responsible for development, which have so far been assigned to the finance minister, and, like my predecessors, I have concerns here that the Minister for Development will have other priorities, that he will not properly take care of the budget and will take particular care of the projects. This could end in serious budgetary problems. The second point I would like to draw attention to is the transfer of ownership powers from the Ministry of the Treasury to ministers, here the Minister responsible for development or economy. So far, we have dealt with a uniform system of supervision, the fact that this law and other acts introduce this supervision into other departments can cause us to lose what was a certain value, namely the uniform principles of ownership supervision. Another thing that threatens us as a result of this operation, which we are carrying out by this law, is the question of mixing two orders - regulatory order and corporate order or the order resulting from the rules of ownership supervision. This also poses a serious risk that the resort, which will exercise regulatory functions, as the owner to whom the company will be more closely supervised, will not be objective in its decisions. But, ladies and gentlemen, we are most concerned about staff issues in this law, because this law is unfortunately emphasising these issues - not on substantive issues, but on staff issues. The provisions of the Act provide that the composition of the Financial Supervision Commission will be extended, that the Board of Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, which has so far been able to count from eight to 12 members, will now count the maximum number, i.e. 12 members. The Management Board of Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, which currently has five members, will be extended to six. Thus, new positions, new costs - we do not find in the law the justification for these costs, we do not find sufficient reasons for the composition of the authorities of this bank and other institutions referred to in the Act to be extended. We fear that Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego, this most important, very important financial institution, which is responsible, among others. for the finances of local governments, budgetary institutions, will also be covered by this negative phenomenon which we observe in companies of the State Treasury, in entities of the State Treasury - a mechanism which we call the replacement of professionals by amateurs, we have dealt with this in horse studs. ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-11-sejm-18-1.u441	There you go. At this point we will also listen to five minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Wojciech Murdzek, the Law and Justice Club, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-11-sejm-18-1.u442	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The aim of the project is to implement two directives of the European Union: the so-called. classic and sectoral. The directives implemented aim to implement the Communication from the European Commission - Europe 2020. A strategy for smart, sustainable and inclusive growth, where public procurement is identified as an instrument to stimulate sustainable and inclusive growth while ensuring the efficient use of public funds. The amendment of the Public Procurement Law is the first stage of the transformation of the public procurement system into a strong instrument for the implementation of the economic and social policy of the State referred to in the "Plan for responsible development of Poland." The new rules concern non-price criteria for the assessment of tenders, the cost of the life cycle of the contract as a criterion for assessing tenders, reducing bureaucratic obligations in proceedings, innovation, increasing the participation of small and medium-sized enterprises in the public procurement market, applying employment contracts and social clauses, in-house procurement. According to the draft price criterion it will be able to weigh more than 60% only if the subject matter of the order is standardised. The strengthening of non-price evaluation criteria should translate into the dissemination of an approach based on a price, quality and operating cost balance. It will be permissible to use the qualification criterion and the experience of the personnel designated for the performance of the contract or the production process criterion. A complete novel will be the possibility to accept a fixed price or cost and, consequently, to evaluate offers only on the basis of non-price criteria. The project provides for the possibility for the contracting authorities to use the life-cycle cost of the contract as a criterion for assessing offers, i.e. not only the cost of acquiring, but also the cost of using, renovating, recycling, etc. One of the main objectives of the project is also to reduce bureaucratic burdens in the process. The purchaser will first be able to assess the tenders and then request documents or statements only from the tenderer whose tender is to be assessed at the most. The project extends the possibility of changing the contract. The provisions of the amendment promote innovation by applying the criterion for assessing innovation-related offers, including by introducing, among others: a new mode of public procurement - an innovative partnership. The project stresses the increase in the participation of small and medium-sized enterprises in the public procurement market through a more flexible division of contracts into smaller ones, the introduction of a ceiling on the company's income which may be required by the contracting authority, the abolition of restrictions on the granting of advances, the strengthening of the possibility for the contracting authority to request the personal execution of key parts of the contract. The project also pursues social policy objectives, in particular the obligation for contracting authorities to require the employment of persons on the basis of a contract of employment, both contractors and subcontractors, to introduce new, extended social clauses enabling the contracting authority to reserve a contract for protected employment establishments and economic operators with at least 30% persons employed are disadvantaged persons. The issue that undoubtedly raises the most controversy is the issue of in-house orders, i.e. contracts awarded by contracting entities to subordinate and controlled entities. The provisions of the revision in this respect are based on the provisions of the Directive, which in turn constitute a codification of the existing case law of the Court of Justice of the European Union, but are not a simple transposition of the provisions of the Directives. According to the draft, the provisions do not constitute an exemption from the application of the law, but are included as a condition for the award of the contract from a free hand. This requires strict disclosure, including the announcement of the intention to conclude a contract and the possibility of appealing against the choice of procedure. Controlled entity, e.g. In order to obtain an order without a tender, the municipal company will have to perform 90% , not 80%, as permitted by the Directive, of the activities in favour of the contracting authority controlling it, e.g. the home municipality. In addition, the draft has been amended to reduce the risk of abuse of in-house orders. The first of the proposed solutions is to limit the possibility of outsourcing contracts concluded in-house to subcontractors. The second proposal is to indicate that the in-house contract expires 3 months after the date on which the private capital was acquired by the controlled legal person that is a party to the contract, except in certain cases. In the third solution [[♪ The bell ♪]] is the proposal to postpone the entry into force of the rules governing in-house contracts, including amendments to the Cleanliness and Order Act in municipalities until 1 January 2017. Thank you very much. I would still like to make one amendment on behalf of the Law and Justice Club. Because of the late hour, maybe I'll put it in writing without reading it anymore.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-11-sejm-18-1.u466	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 2 May, as every year, we celebrated Flag Day of the Republic of Poland. The Flag Day of the Republic of Poland was established in 2004, by the Act of 20 February 2004 amending the Law on emblems, colours and hymns of the Republic of Poland. The choice of that day was not accidental, because on 2 May 1945 Polish soldiers who conquered the capital of Nazi Germany hung a white-red flag on the column of victory and on the Reichstag, which represented a symbolic ending of war activities. In addition, 2 May is preceded by one of the most important national holidays of our country, the celebration of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland commemorating the announcement of the Constitution of the Republic of Poland on 3 May 1791. Moreover, the establishment of the Flag Day of the Republic of Poland on 2 May is justified in the operation of communist authorities in the times of the Polish People's Republic, when on that day state flags were removed on the occasion of the celebration of Labor Day on 1 May, so that they were not visible on the day of the abolition by the communist authorities of the feast of the Constitution on 3 May. One of the main reasons why the initiators of the establishment of the 2nd May Flag Day of the Republic of Poland were the popularization of knowledge about Polish identity and national symbols. The colors on the national flag are one of the most important elements of national identity. Historically Polish national colours are derived from the coat of arms of the Kingdom of Poland, as well as the coat of arms of the Grand Duchy of Lithuania. According to the explanation of the symbolism of the Polish flag white comes from the white eagle which is the emblem of Poland and the white Pogoni - a knight galloping on a horse, which is the emblem of Lithuania. Both of these emblems are on the red backgrounds of the coat of arms. For this reason, Polish flag of white was placed at the top, because in the heraldyka the color of the emblem is more important than the background. It is worth mentioning here that long before the official establishment of the Polish flag, white and red colors were considered as national colours, while the first information about official national colours appeared only in the legislation of the Republic of Poland in 1831. However, it was necessary to wait much longer for the official appearance of the Polish flag, because Poland was under partitions. It was officially established only in 1919 and has since changed its colors several times. Everything was clearly defined in 1980 when the colour of the Polish flag was finally established. The day of the flag is one of the youngest national holidays, the more it should be promoted and confirmed by our respect for the Polish national symbol. However, I would like to share with you my observation about the need to show due respect for the Polish flag not only on the day of the flag celebration and on the occasion of other state holidays, but also on weekdays. For some time I have been accompanied by the conviction that we are still not doing enough to protect our national symbol. Many times we witness events in which the Polish flag is used in an inappropriate way, without the respect that Polish national symbols should be shown. Polish emblem and Polish flag are our symbols and elements of national identity. Through the white and red flag we express our emotions, not only on the days of the flag's holidays, but also on a daily basis. That is why it is so important to continue to promote the values and importance of our national symbols and to educate children from an early age. The actions taken by the state and local authorities of all levels are therefore necessary to strengthen respect for our national symbols and mobilise society to increasingly engage in the cultivation of Polish national identity. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-11-sejm-18-1.u467	There you go. That concludes our parliamentary statements.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-11-sejm-18-1.u468	Declaration on the 225th anniversary of the adoption of the Constitution on 3 May. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! May 3rd this year We celebrated the 225th anniversary of the Constitution on 3 May. It is widely accepted that it was the first in Europe and the second in the world (after the American Constitution of 1787) a modern, written constitution. The Constitution on 3 May was to respond to the deteriorating internal and international situation of the Republic of Poland, which 150 years earlier was one of the largest European powers and the largest country in Europe. One of the main reasons for the collapse of the country was the abuse of Liberum veto law. Since 1652 it has allowed every Member to break up the Sejm and reject all adopted resolutions. By taking advantage of this possibility, the magnates, and even the agents working for the neighbouring countries, could easily bribe one or more people to reject any inconvenient reform from their point of view. However, events in the world have created an atmosphere conducive to reformers. The Polish neighbours were engaged in wars (especially with the Ottoman Empire) and in solving internal conflicts, therefore they did not interfere in Polish matters. The four-year Sejm, which was held in 1788 and 1792, created the greatest potential for reform. It was called. The Confederated Seym, which was free of liberum veto, allowed for the adoption of radical for the time of reform. The authors of the draft constitution were, in particular, Ignacy Potocki, King Stanisław August Poniatowski and Fr. Hugo Kołłętaj. Following the disclosure of the text, the opposition focused on the Russian ambassador became active, so reform supporters decided to bring forward a draft basic law in order to adopt it at a one-day session, which violated the rules of the parliament. The historic session of the joint chambers sitting in the Senator's Hall at the Royal Castle was full of violent disputes. In the end, however, the king swore a constitution, without reading it, to the hands of Bishop Felix Turski of Cracow. On 7 May the Marshals of the Sejm issued a universal proclamation of the Constitution. The Constitution left the state system in Poland. However, the influence of magnateria on the country's management was reduced by excluding nobles without land from the seymists. In the electoral law, the census of possession replaced the previous feudal census. The locals were granted the right to acquire earthly goods and to obtain nobles. The Constitution maintained the servitude of the peasants, but deprived the nobles of the law of the highest authority. The Constitution's provisions centralized the State. The separation between the Crown and Lithuania has been abolished. A unified government, treasure, and army were introduced. Catholicism was recognized as a religion, with tolerance for other religions. The Constitution also introduced a threefold division of power: the legislative power was to be exercised by a two-room parliament consisting of the Sejm - 204 MEPs from among the nobles of the mansions and 24 plenipotentiaries of the cities - and the Senate. Message instructions, confederations and veto liberum have been removed. The executive authority was given to the king along with the council, the so-called. They're law enforcement. Free election was abolished - after the death of Stanisław August Poniatowski, the throne was to be hereditary. The Constitution was valid only for a year before it was overthrown by the Russian army along with the trade union. Since its adoption, however, it has become a symbol of efforts to regain the independence and sovereignty of the Republic of Poland, regardless of internal obstacles and external threats. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-05-11-sejm-18-1.u469	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On May 8, we celebrated the 71st anniversary of the end of World War II. World War II, these three simple words in sentence, symbolize the unimaginable victims, suffering, disability, the misery of hundreds of millions of people around the world. The day of the end of World War II is often referred to as the day of victory. How bitter it was to win our nation. During the war, 6 million Poles were murdered. Our country suffered the greatest sacrifice among the fighting, one-sixth nation perished. Poland was the first country to attack Nazi Germany. Despite heroic defenses - an example is 9. Grayev horse ranger regiment, our regiment of Łomża land, who fought as one of the few since September 1939 until the last battle in the September campaign near Kock on October 5, 1939 - our country was defeated. Another example of bravery and self - sacrificing is the defence of the Vizna, also called the Polish Thermopylae. Despite the great advantage of the enemy, i.e. 40 to 1, Captain's squad. Raginis stopped General Guderian's armor corps for three days. The Battle of Vizna is a relatively little known episode of the September campaign, but its importance and brave resistance of the Polish soldier appreciated in the diaries our opponent General Guderian. Today's heroism of defenders is presented by the Swedish team Sabaton. It is a very meaningful devotion to heroes that musicians from a foreign country, Sweden, have created a work about defending the Vizna. Our allies England and France did not give us any effective help, as they were obliged to do. The knife in the back of Poland was stabbed by Bolshevik Russia on 17 September 1939, attacking our eastern borders and thus breaking all treaties and agreements. The land of Łomża under the agreement of Ribbentrop - Molotov was under Soviet occupation. This period is often called the first Soviet occupation. Our land, our little homeland, was called West Belarus. The Lomzyniacs were transported massively into Soviet Russia. Their only fault was that they were Poles. On inhuman soil they died of exhaustion and diseases, forced by the Bolshevik system to work in the Soviet Łagrads. The Lomzyniacs fought against the Nazi invader and died on all fronts of World War II. Poles were murdered in the Katowice Gestapo and NKVD prisons. The great patriotism and courage are borne out by numerous guerrilla troops that were established on the land of Łomża. This is captain Victor Konopka, commander of the recreated 9. The regiment of Home Army horsemen fought the biggest guerrilla battle in the Białystok region under the village of Grzy. The brave commander died in battle. On 8 May 1945 the act of Nazi surrender of Germany was signed - the war was over. The Nazi Germans, who sought to exterminate the Polish people, were defeated. Our country, even though it fought for the longest time and suffered huge sacrifices, was not free. With the consent of the Western allies who sold us to Stalin in Yalta, Poland became a vassal - a satellite of the Soviet Union. The communist system was imposed on Poland. The Communists, through fraudulent elections, legalized a new, alien, force-forced system. The new power sought to eliminate people who did not support the current system. The prison in Łomża was filled with political and social activists, soldiers. Often the heroes of the conspiracy and guerrilla activities were imprisoned for one purpose with the criminals of the SS. In the new reality, there were court murders on the agenda. Among other things, General Emil Fielddorf and Captain Witold Pilecki were murdered. Among my fellow men, however, there were people who could resist communist tyranny with a gun in their hand. When the world celebrated the end of World War II, the guerrilla unit under the command of Major. Jan Tabortowski's pseudo. During the night of the 8th to 9th of May, the breeze carried out an effective attack on Grajewo, releasing the prisoners. The last anti-communist guerrilla - a soldier cursed on the land of Łomża Stanisław Marchewka pseudod. The fish was murdered by security in 1957. Only in 1989, after 6 years of World War II and 44 years of communist enslavement, did our country obtain the desired freedom. Poland was the first to break out of the communist block, thus setting an example to other enslaved countries of this sick system. My generation, glory to God, does not know what war is, but from my parents' memories, my grandparents, we can imagine what war was. War is death, suffering and misery. Each of us should work as far as possible for the benefit of the country, for its development, to improve its economy and thus strengthen the Armed Forces. As history teaches, no alliance can guarantee 100% security - some alliances were only on paper. We Poles, like no nation in the world, value freedom because we know exactly how much it cost our ancestors. In order to fully appreciate the freedom we have, we must remember our culture, our traditions of our small homelands, cultivate them, and never forget our ancestors who gave their lives in defence of our homeland. Thank you.
